A Proposal to a Table","volume":"婚后外传：公爵与写错的新郎","anchor":"chapter-0212","paragraphs":["The organiser asked him not to agitate the waiting guests further. The false host offered Mara a made-up appointment time, then tried to withhold the receipt until she paid. She did not. Cassian stood beside her with the amiable patience of a man enjoying someone else's bad afternoon. The organiser showed the venue's real booking message. A variety of explanations followed. None convincingly explained why the money went to the host's personal account.","He alleged that the private fee would improve the lot of the charity's children. The organiser asked him to stop using that claim. Under-nutrition was not a marketing device that made every collection legitimate. The programme's donation had a named recipient and an agreed record. The pretender was not accountable for either. He was simply asking strangers to trust a badge. The police were called, and the organiser stayed with the affected guests.","Mara did not need to hunt down a grand conspiracy. The hijacked media page had advertised this specific reception, and people were waiting here. A body of messages linked them to the same offer. That might bear on a complaint; it did not let her deduce every hidden intention. Cassian kept her company while staff arranged refunds where possible and explained where to report the rest. No point in a combative exchange should deplete the crew before the event began. Their mutual wish was chiefly to keep a bad discovery from becoming an unpleasant crowd.","A few messages had come from readers around the world. The response was all too familiar to people who organised launches. Back ashore, Zara found enough mania for the real event to make the foyer noisy. The atmospheric hall had ample light and a small garden. Guests paced back and forth, carrying books whose on-line ads had become unexpectedly controversial. The publisher's dynamic assistant rewrote the notice. Indirectly, a clear paragraph could knock out several rumours before they reached the microphone. It could not erase the money some guests had lost.","Zara would not withhold her own statement. She apologised for the confusion, described the genuine programme and named the false offer. She did not apologise for a theft she had not committed. Her words were cohesive and fine-grained enough to let visitors distinguish one from the other. A sceptical man asked whether he was entitled to leave. She was doubtful he would want to stay, but she would not disparage that choice. The organiser said yes and explained the ticket terms. Nobody was made to stay as proof of faith.","In the workshop, a young writer complained that the language exercise was abnormal. Zara asked what he meant. The case-ending was different from English, not randomly broken. Its ending would be similar to a sign telling the reader which character received a parcel. A body of examples could make that clear. Her invented geo-centric cosmos likewise had rules; fanciful did not mean incoherent. It was a playground for an idea, not a replacement for astronomy.","Cassian tried to formulate a sentence in the imaginary language. He married the table. The youngest writer was way ahead and had drawn a cute picture of the mistaken bride. Mara pulled an exaggerated face. The children urged Cassian to gurn back, and the class laughed when he tried. The man who could open a locked door had been defeated by grammar. He asked Zara to prioritize a divorce. Her supreme delight in the request made the moment enjoyable enough to reclaim the room from its earlier worry.","The cardboard stage door came loose, and a helper had to unhinge it before it fell. The interruption could depress a nervous actor; Cassian bowed to it. The next discussion concerned the constitutionality of the king's decree. Zara could explain it abstractly, but the made-up law became clearer when they acted the scene. A middle-aged, childless monarch was being pressured to name a successor. The court's answer would bear on his private life. Cassian played the king with such wounded dignity that Mara refused to support his favourite candidate merely because she liked his face.","A participant thought this a biased choice. \"Yes,\" she said. \"I am trying to behave unlike the heroine.\"","Light could diffuse through the frosted glass without making the room dark. An enraged king could be funny without making the actor cruel. Cassian agreed to go along with that idea. The class was amused. Anecdotal reactions did not comprise a formal test of the book, though Zara valued them. Peer review of a research paper would ask different questions. Stories would invariably exert an influence on a reader’s expectations, but she could not generalize from six comments to everybody. Her writing partner had been instrumental in catching contradictions. She was not going to call every friendly conversation a substitute for that work. Some degree of distinction kept the claims modest.","The printer arrived with copies whose new cover was associated with the story's actual ending. His credit term had allowed the small press a little time, and he was not demanding payment in return for a public compliment. The economic boom had filled the town with expensive launches. This one used volunteer flowers, tea and cakes. To improve the lot of a small press did not require pretending it was a luxury brand.","On the basis of the workshop, Zara would reevaluate the handout. Its success in places had been astounding; in others, she was in need of simpler examples. The alphabetical signing order still put Zara last. She called the habit alphabetism with enough humour that two other Z-named writers joined her. They could break up the sequence by drawing lots next year. Anyhow, nobody was asking for a constitutional amendment. The written exercise about thousands of years hence now contained three imaginary futures and a picture of a married table. Zara promised to keep the picture.","The incident would not refocus itself without a clear notice. By sunset, the panic had begun to evaporate. Mara watched Cassian turn the page of the novel she had refused him that morning. He was acutely absorbed. \"Do you like him?\" she asked.","\"Limitedly,\" he said. \"His wife is astoundingly difficult.\"","She kissed him behind the book, and his next complaint was lost."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1040,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0212\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵与写错的新郎</div><h1>2. The Author Takes Her Bow","volume":"婚后外传：公爵与写错的新郎","anchor":"chapter-0213","paragraphs":["At the evening reading, Zara asked Cassian to play the villain. He objected that the book had contained insufficient preparation for his marriage to a table. Mara said the performance would be similar to normal dinner conversation. He had the supreme dignity to ignore her, then took the stage with a coat over one shoulder and a look that made three guests sit straighter.","The atmospheric room was full, with ample space on the small stage. Readers around the world had sent encouraging messages. A body of listeners had come ashore from the late ferry, bringing a variety of battered copies. Its small economic boom could not be measured in luxury bookings. It was the sudden sale of a book the publisher had nearly postponed. The credit term would expire soon, and actual sales mattered. Nobody needed to call that mundane fact unworthy of art.","Zara was a middle-aged, childless writer whose private life had too often been treated as an incomplete story. Tonight she had a novel to read. The villain's romantic mania was fanciful, but his acute concern for the heroine was convincingly written. A soft lamp would diffuse the light across their faces. When Cassian delivered his first line, Mara felt an entirely biased satisfaction. The made-up man might be handsome. The real one was hers.","The platform team had secured the hijacked media page. The false ads had been removed, though copied screenshots remained. Zara's notice would stay on-line with the genuine event details. The organiser was accountable for answering new questions and had assigned someone that task. Indirectly, the new notice could exert an influence on later decisions without magically reaching everyone. Some degree of unfinished work remained after the enjoyable part began.","The invisible fastening in Mara’s collar came loose, and Cassian fixed it shortly before the reading began. The play comprised three scenes. A cute paper crown passed back and forth between the actors. Each had to refocus after the laughter. The scene involved a sealed letter and a misunderstood case-ending. Mara could deduce the misunderstanding from the letter. She played the heroine, initially misreading who had received the proposal. Cassian had to gurn through three increasingly absurd explanations while refusing to admit he had written it. The grammar joke did not require every guest to know the invented language. A fine-grained introduction made the difference clear, and a cohesive performance carried the rest. Zara's preparation had been instrumental in letting them be foolish convincingly.","The heroine then questioned the constitutionality of an edict demanding marriage. A fanciful court with a geo-centric worldview was still capable of a specific political argument. Zara resisted explaining it abstractly from the stage. The character needed something; the law stood between her and it. That was enough to agitate the scene. A made-up constitution could bear on a real feeling without claiming to govern the audience.","At the end, the hero chose to break up his council rather than let it choose his wife. The plot would not let hope evaporate merely because the future remained uncertain. The small gesture did not improve the lot of every person in his kingdom. It solved the story he was in. Zara had been acutely wary of writing a romantic decision as universal rescue. The heroine would go along with the plan because she wanted him, not in return for being saved from every future difficulty.","Mara came off stage laughing. Cassian could not withhold his pleasure at her delight. He kissed her hand while the audience clapped. \"I feel my threat to hunt down the author was premature,\" he said.","\"Do not disparage my villain.\"","\"I should reevaluate him. Limitedly.\"","The amiable writer had returned to the signing table. Her book was associated with an astounding afternoon she had never planned. A sceptical guest asked whether the police complaint was a publicity stunt. The organiser answered on the basis of the actual messages, without sharing personal details. The predominant story did not have to be the most sinister interpretation. An angry fan could be mistaken, and an anecdotal account could still require checking.","An abnormal day had ended well. They could knock out the redundant registration questions later. The group would formulate a better notice for future events. The sign-up list would not invariably follow the alphabet. Alphabetism had started as Zara's schoolroom joke and now named an all too familiar habit they could change without heroics. They would prioritize clear access over decorative mystery. Anyhow, Zara liked the drawing of the married table better than most of the official forms.","A final prompt asked visitors what they would preserve thousands of years hence. One chose the story, one a recipe, one the memory of a man trying to negotiate with a table. Mara would not let an abstract future take up the entire evening. She wrote nothing. She had no need to amass a theoretical answer to the future before enjoying the person beside her. A child asked if they could play the scene again. She promised to discuss it with the newly divorced duke.","The false reception could not wholly unhinge Zara that had so many real people repairing it. The dynamic changed when the guests stopped waiting for a perfect host and began sharing what they liked. A peer review discussion in another room had asked more specialised questions. These guests were entitled to enjoy a story without taking an examination. Their mutual delight was no less real. That did not deplete the organiser's responsibilities. It made room for pleasure beside them. The middle-class audience did not need to be treated as helpless consumers, and Zara had no wish to be their flawless guide.","At home, Cassian let the book fall open on his chest. Mara took it before he could fall asleep on it. His determination might depress the bedtime prospects of a man who had said he was tired. His obsession had become gratifyingly inconvenient: he wanted to know whether the heroine loved the villain from the beginning.","\"She was doubtful,\" Mara said. \"Then he improved.\"","\"A cruelly slow courtship.\"","She turned out the light and kissed him in the dark. \"Worth the next chapter.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1031,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0213\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵与写错的新郎</div><h1>3. A Refreshing Definition","volume":"婚后外传：雨中的小岛与公爵的帽子","anchor":"chapter-0214","paragraphs":["The island guide promised a refreshing walk. Mara asked whether refreshing meant a view or a climb that would make her curse. He said both. Cassian offered his arm and the wicked smile she should have learnt to distrust. She accepted without any hesitation at all. Someday, she intended to make him carry her up a hill merely because he had suggested it.","They had travelled from the mainland to a Mediterranean isle for a small astronomy weekend. The guide's dog tried to lick every arriving hand. Its owner had to scoop up the terrier before it followed a child onto the luggage trolley. The group included a talkative private member of parliament, Leda, who held no government office and hoped to remain a tourist for forty-eight hours. Her aide had not come. She called the absence unimaginable luxury.","A retired lecturer from Columbia had brought a mobile display. One panel compared a planet with an asteroid; another showed a comet in a dark sky. A small model labelled one particle electron and another neutron. Mara did not need to master the cosmos before lunch. She did enjoy watching Cassian plough through the quiz with the grave concentration of a schoolboy. He was willing to admit that the universe had survived remarkably well without his advice.","Their coastal route followed a stretch of low rock, then passed a brook and a pond before climbing toward the cape. The mountain above the highland path was lost in cloud. A bird’s nest sat beneath a sheltered stone lip. A shallow cave opened above a steep bank; its visible cavity held old seaweed, not a secret hoard. A waterfall crossed the valley beyond it after heavy rain. The guide pointed out the sound rock line and the softer ground beside a swamp. His geological story made the terrain more interesting without offering a challenge to scramble anywhere they pleased.","Leda had to sit for a history examination later in the year. She was studying local courses because public office had interrupted her degree. She asked the lecturer to enrich the walk with questions. He was a peer reviewer of field guides and, unlike a bad host, did not confuse curiosity with a test nobody had agreed to take. On the basis of an old photo, he showed where a glacier had once filled a distant valley on the continent. That picture was from Europe, far from this warm little shore.","The guide hoped to upgrade the route cards in the near future. He invited visitors to refer to the map. The guide would not invoke the old volcano as an excuse to make every pebble exciting. Ordinary rock could fascinate a visitor too. The island's bedrock told part of its story, while a map showed the peninsula and the narrow gulf between their path and the next headland. They had crossed from seaside village to grass without leaving the marked trail. Mara skimmed the map, then looked up to take in the actual view.","The crux of the afternoon would be timing the return before the weather changed. The first rain began to spit as they reached the plateau. Clouds threatened to close the high route. The guide had expected a change, though the speed was variable. He turned them toward the lower path rather than overburden the party with a forced climb. Cassian took Mara's bag. She had brought proper boots; he had brought an on-trend hat that behaved like a sail. The hat was mighty fine, Leda said, apparently hoping to amuse them. She promised not to deride it until they were indoors.","\"A naive pledge,\" he said.","\"An accurate assessment.\"","A gust took the hat and sent it into a tangle of branches. The guide's terrier followed before its owner could call it back. Cassian retrieved the dog first, then surrendered the hat to a branch. His choice made Mara want to kiss him on the spot. She settled for holding his hand while the guide secured the lead. The dog repaid him with a thorough lick of rainwater and affection.","They moved downhill beside a canal cut long ago to drain the garden. The stream's foam turned white around a fallen branch. One bubble rose through the wet stone pool. A lone ember glowed in a sheltered barbecue pit beside the path; the guide's colleague poured water over it and stayed until it was out. The weather could still threaten to make an ordinary mistake expensive. They had no need to surpass a modest afternoon's ambition.","Water surged forth from the lower brook. The sea struck the rocks in one wave after another below them. Beyond the next bend, a sudden slide of wet earth covered the path. It was not enormous, but it was moving. The guide called the party back from the incline. Mara felt Cassian's fingers tighten around hers, then release so they could move freely. Their mere glance had not shown the force below. A rapid trickle became a brown stream across the track.","The route to the mainland landing lay beyond it. Their own side held the cave, a dry ledge and the guide's working radio. It would be foolish to treat that as a disaster before knowing more, and equally naive to pretend the path was still open. Mara pulled her coat close. An unreal second of silence gave way to the sound of rain."],"questions":[],"wordCount":902,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0214\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：雨中的小岛与公爵的帽子</div><h1>1. A Seat in the Universe","volume":"婚后外传：雨中的小岛与公爵的帽子","anchor":"chapter-0215","paragraphs":["The mobile phone had no useful signal here. The cave was wide enough for the group and dry beyond the entrance. It had no mysterious second passage. The guide checked the visible floor, then brought them onto the level ledge away from the wet bank. A small cavity in the wall held a bird's abandoned nest. Mara sat beside it while Cassian folded his coat beneath her. She protested, and he said that someday she might allow him one thoroughly predictable pleasure.","Rain continued to spit across the cape. The waterfall beyond the brook had grown louder, and the swamp below shone through the grass. Their Mediterranean isle looked less like a postcard now. The mainland boat would wait until the landing route was checked. A mere delay was preferable to making someone cross moving ground. The crux was waiting for a route, not proving who could walk fastest. The waterfall made a mighty noise. The guide used his radio to tell the landing team exactly where the party was.","Leda, the private member trying to remain anonymous, offered her phone in case another network worked. It did not. She could not invoke a title to improve the signal. Leda called their sudden cave tea party an unimaginable luxury under the circumstances. The lecturer from Columbia had a map and spare light. He could enrich the wait by telling a story or be quiet. The talkative guest had become quiet enough to hear the rain. The party voted for both in turn. It was a refreshing decision, and one that required no committee papers.","The geological map needed an upgrade before next season. He unrolled the panel about the glacier. The caption named Europe; his cover portrait had been taken in Africa. The image showed a valley on a distant continent, not this cave. The scale was easy to misunderstand if he let the frame conceal the caption. Mara was willing to admit that she had first thought the ice belonged here. The camera’s angle was deceptive, and its red ink had begun to bleed where rain struck the edge. He would refer to the caption before discussing the scene. He would not deride a question prompted by his own poor placement. A good peer reviewer, he said, noticed where a reader could reasonably go wrong.","The little terrier tried to lick the picture. Its owner moved it away, and Cassian bent to scoop up the dog before it settled on the paper. The resulting photograph was wholly on-trend: an elegant man holding a wet animal with complete seriousness. Mara promised not to publish it without asking. He told her she was being wicked, which was true only in intention. The dog looked delighted by its new throne.","The panel about a planet and an asteroid remained rolled in its tube. Questions arrived in one wave after another. A child asked whether a comet could knock them off the island. The lecturer answered the question plainly without turning the rain into a lesson in terror. The cosmos was large; today's problem was local water and earth. Electron and neutron labels on his little teaching model could wait. He was not going to overburden a frightened boy with more information than he had requested.","The guide brought forth a flask and cups. Mara skimmed the surface of her drink with a spoon to remove a leaf that had fallen in. Cassian offered bread. She took its crust and fed the terrier only the small piece its owner approved. No bubble of forced cheer could change the weather, but warm hands and food made the wait easier. The foam on the brook was beginning to thin.","The wet lead made a small tangle at Cassian’s feet; he freed it before anyone moved. A moving patch of light on the water resembled an ember. Mara watched it until Cassian touched her cheek. She realised she was cold enough to shiver. He wrapped the dry inner layer of his coat around her and pulled her close. She understood the care without any explanation at all. His lips brushed her temple, and for a moment the universe narrowed to a place inside his arms.","The dark shape of the old volcano remained visible through the cloud. The peninsula path was being inspected from the far side. Their guide knew another approach across the mountain shoulder, but he would not try it before hearing from his colleague. To plough into unfamiliar mud merely because one disliked waiting would be naive. The party could surpass its own expectations by sitting still. Leda found this understandable and considerably harder than it sounded.","She took out a notebook to prepare for the examination she would sit for in autumn. She had intended to work predominantly on the history of the seaside settlement. The guide pointed out a safe view of its old wall beyond the pond. On the basis of the map, she could relate it to the gulf and the lower plateau. The geography had stopped being a series of nouns and become a place she was waiting in.","At last the radio spoke. The downhill route was closed, but the upper service lane was sound. Two staff members would meet them at the next marked junction. The clouds still threatened to bring another shower. The walk would be longer and comparatively steep in one section. There was no rush. The rain had begun to ease, and a pale rainbow appeared beyond the cape.","Mara stood slowly. \"Come on,\" she told Cassian. \"I promised to laugh at your hat indoors.\"","\"A promise I trust you to keep.\"","He took her hand, and they went toward the light together."],"questions":[],"wordCount":950,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0215\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：雨中的小岛与公爵的帽子</div><h1>2. →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x040c03","title":"3. The Hat Comes Home","volume":"婚后外传：雨中的小岛与公爵的帽子","anchor":"chapter-0216","paragraphs":["The guide found a pebble by the shelter and put it safely off the walking line. The bedrock here gave them firmer footing. The service lane rose from the cave above the brook, crossing a steep slope before easing onto firm ground. A staff member walked at the front and another behind. Their guide kept the terrier on its lead. Cassian remained beside Mara, close enough to catch her if needed and wise enough not to make a ceremony of every step. The private member followed with the map rolled beneath her arm, enjoying the unimaginable satisfaction of being simply another muddy walker.","From the ridge, the Mediterranean isle spread below them. The cape looked smaller, the swamp no longer looked like a lake and the waterfall was a white thread across the valley. Beyond it, the mainland appeared through a break in the cloud. Mara watched a gull skim the water. The little bird knew nothing of their temporary adventure and had no intention of treating it as the centre of the universe.","The lecturer from Columbia stopped by a shelter with his colleague's permission. His peer reviewer had once criticised an early draft for making every journey sound like an examination. He had taken the comment seriously. He would not amuse people by inventing an exciting danger. A field course could offer a refreshing picture of a continent without forcing every walker to name the rock. Today, he would let the glacier photograph rest and ask whether anyone needed water.","Leda helped collect the cups. Someday she might tell the story at a public meeting. It would be easy to turn a sensible wait into a heroic speech. She preferred to remember the guide who kept an anxious group informed. The land had not become safe because a private member was watching. The safe route had existed, and the people who maintained it had found the right time to use it.","At the village, the group returned to the classroom to dry off. A model planet sat beside the tube holding the asteroid panel. The comet picture glowed on a screen. Mara helped the child arrange an electron label and a neutron label beside the correct teaching pieces, following the printed key rather than pretending expertise. The cosmos could still fascinate him after a wet afternoon. It did not have to overshadow the fact that he had been frightened.","Shortly before supper, Cassian found his hat waiting by the radiator. A staff member had retrieved it from the tangle after the wind dropped. He put a finger over the small hole in its crown. Its brim now looked predominantly like an unhappy mushroom. Mara was incapable of mercy. \"On-trend,\" she said. \"For woodland spirits.\"","\"I will enrich your wardrobe with it.\"","\"Come on. You wouldn't.\"","His wicked smile was all the answer she needed. She tried to flee behind a chair; he caught her at the waist, kissed her until she laughed and placed the hat on her head. The child declared it beautiful. This opinion was understandable but not wholly trustworthy.","It was naive to suppose every wet day had to be a failure. The crux of the evening was whether anyone wanted to leave this warm room. The guide invited them to supper. Its bread had a thick crust and came from the same oven as the afternoon loaf. Mara watched a single ember in the hearth while the cook covered the fire. The practical task was finished before he joined the table. He would not overburden a departing assistant by leaving everything for her. Small decisions could make an ordinary job easier without becoming a speech about virtue.","A toy plough decorated the windowsill, carved by a grandfather who once farmed the plateau. The child asked why it belonged beside a picture of Europe. The farmer had travelled there years ago, the guide said. Pictures could preserve two different histories without making them one. The old man's photograph from Africa hung nearby, while a map of Asia stood behind his books. The room was a collection of places somebody had loved, not a claim to have mastered the planet.","The rain began to spit again. A mere wet sleeve could not ruin the evening. The tour agent offered an upgrade to the next day's boat trip after the interruption. Mara thanked her, then asked for a quiet morning instead. Their enjoyment need not surpass today's inconvenience on a score sheet. They could take a walk to the beach without any timetable at all. A new version of the weather might bring sun or more rain; both deserved a coat.","The former guide training room had an old skull model in a cabinet. The lecturer pointed out its cavity only because the child asked; he did not drag the entire group back into class. The boy then wanted to know whether the terrier's brain was full of plans to lick supper plates. The owner said it certainly appeared so. Cassian had to scoop up the hopeful animal before it reached a chair.","Laughter burst forth when the terrier found the hat again. The storm would no longer threaten to close the returning lane, though staff would inspect it again before the morning walk. The guide said they could upgrade the written notice after today's experience. An on-trend photograph mattered less than a useful map when someone needed the way home. Mara nodded, too content to mock her husband again.","That night she stood at the window while he lit the bedside lamp. The cave, the rain and his warm coat had become one tired, bright memory. She turned when he said her name. Someday, she thought, she would know the best word for the way his voice made a room become home.","For now, she crossed it and kissed him."],"questions":[],"wordCount":974,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0216\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：雨中的小岛与公爵的帽子</div><h1>3.
